, defined by:
{N^{\alpha}}\sim{O^{\gamma}}
if
{N^{\alpha}}={O^{\gamma}}
or
{O^{\gamma}}\in\mathcal{L}({N^{\alpha}})
, is an equivalence relation on
\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}
, partitioning it into a collection of layers. The fact that all loops in
\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}
are weak then allows us to say that a given layer either only contains green dashed arrows between its elements (in which case we will call it a green layer), or only contains red dashed arrows (in which case we will call it a red layer)
20
20

20

Note that single-branch layers can be considered to be either green or red: the choice will not affect the proof.
.

Furthermore, merging the nodes of each layer transforms
\Gamma^{\texttt{Bran}}
into an acyclic graph. One can thus define a partial order between layers. Arbitrarily turning it into a total order, and picking arbitrary orderings within each layer, leads to a total ordering
<
of
\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}
in which branches of a same layer are all next to each other, and in which
{N^{\alpha}}<{O^{\gamma}}\implies{N^{\alpha}}\not\in{\mathcal{F}^{\textrm{str}}}({O^{\gamma}})
. We can use this total ordering to label the branches with natural numbers, as
\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}=\{B(i)\,|\,1\leq i\leq n\}
. For a given
i
and a given branch
{N^{\alpha}}>B(i)
, we define
{\mathcal{P}_{i}}({N^{\alpha}}):=\mathcal{P}({N^{\alpha}})\cap\{{O^{\gamma}}>B(i)\}
,
{\mathcal{F}_{i}}({N^{\alpha}}):=\mathcal{F}({N^{\alpha}})\cap\{{O^{\gamma}}>B(i)\}
, etc.

B.4.2
The induction hypothesis

This ordering of
\Gamma
’s branches will allows us to define an induction. The point is to start from
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}]
, and then to ‘refill’ the branches one by one, making sure that the unitary obtained at each step is sufficiently well-behaved to be able to move to the next step. To define it, we will first need to define these ‘partially filled exchanges’ that are being used at every step
i
in the induction, which we shall call
V_{N,i}
’s. We do so by defining how they act on each branch: i.e.,
\forall i,\forall{N^{\alpha}}
, we define
V_{N,i}^{\alpha}:\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{in}}_{N}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{\beta|{N^{\beta}}>B(i)}\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{out}}_{N^{\beta}}\right)\to\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{out}}_{N}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{\beta|{N^{\beta}}>B(i)}\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{in}}_{N^{\beta}}\right)
by

V_{N,i}^{\alpha}=\begin{cases}i_{N,\textrm{pad}}^{\alpha}\circ\left(\textsc{swap}_{{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{in}}},{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{out}}}}\otimes(\bigotimes_{\beta>B(i),\beta\neq\alpha}\Theta_{N^{\beta}})\right)\circ p_{N,\textrm{pad}}^{\alpha}&\textrm{ if }{N^{\alpha}}>B(i)\,,\\
\left(i_{N}^{\alpha}\circ{U_{N}^{\alpha}}\circ p_{N}^{\alpha}\right)\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{\beta>B(i)}\Theta_{N^{\beta}}\right)&\textrm{ if }{N^{\alpha}}\leq B(i)\,,\end{cases}

(48)

and we use them to define

V_{N,i}:=\sum_{\alpha\in\texttt{Bran}({\lambda_{N}})}V_{N,i}^{\alpha}\,.

(49)

We will write the input (resp. output) space of
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]
as
\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\textrm{out}}:=\mathcal{H}_{P}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{N^{\beta}}>B(i)}\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{out}}_{{N^{\beta}}}\right)
(resp.
\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\textrm{in}}:=\mathcal{H}_{F}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{N^{\beta}}>B(i)}\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{in}}_{{N^{\beta}}}\right)
). We also write
\bar{V}_{N,i}^{\alpha}:=V_{N,i}-V_{N,i}^{\alpha}
. Note that the
V_{N,i}
’s follow the
{\lambda_{N}}
’s by construction, and that one has
V_{N,0}=\textsc{exch}_{N}
and
V_{N,n}=U_{N}
.

The core of the induction will be the hypothesis that, at step
i
,
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]
is unitary. However, this will not be sufficient: we will also need other conditions ensuring that
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]
features structural properties which allow us to move to step
i+1
. More precisely, these conditions will encode the fact that at every step
i
, and for every branch
{N^{\alpha}}
that hasn’t been filled yet (i.e. such that
{N^{\alpha}}>B(i)
), one can find projectors on
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]
’s inputs and outputs that control whether
{N^{\alpha}}
happens or not, and that all these projectors will play well with one another.

One subtlety is that, if
B(i)
is in a
red
layer and if there are still unfilled branches in that layer, then the projectors controlling the status of branches above that layer cannot be defined. This is ultimately not problematic, as one can wait for the whole layer to have been filled to redefine them; but this will force us to amend parts of the induction hypothesis when it is the case.

Finally, another part of the induction hypothesis will rely on the causal properties of
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(V_{N,i})_{N}\right]
. We will describe these by using the behaviour of
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]
seen as an isomorphism of operator algebras, defining
\mathcal{V}_{i}:\Lin\left[\mathcal{H}_{P}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}>B(i)}\mathcal{H}_{{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}}\right)\right]\to\Lin\left[\mathcal{H}_{F}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}>B(i)}\mathcal{H}_{{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{in}}}}\right)\right]
by

\forall f,\mathcal{V}_{i}[f]:=\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\circ f\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]^{\dagger}\,.

(50)

When
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]
is unitary, this defines an isomorphism of operator algebras, preserving sums, compositions, and the dagger. This implies that, more generally,
\mathcal{V}_{i}
will preserve commutation relations, self-adjointness, idempotency, etc.

We now turn to our induction hypotheses at step
i
.

Induction Hypothesis 1

(H1)
.

\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]
is unitary.

As we mentioned, H1 is the core of the induction, and will allow us to conclude in the end that
\mathcal{S}[(V_{N,n})_{N}]=\mathcal{S}[(U_{N})_{N}]
is indeed unitary.

Induction Hypothesis 2

(H2)
.

One has defined, for all
{N^{\alpha}}>B(i)
, orthogonal projectors:

•

{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, acting on
\begin{cases}\mathcal{H}_{P}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}_{i}}({N^{\alpha}})}\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\,&\textrm{ if }{N^{\alpha}}\textrm{ is in a green layer;}\\
\mathcal{H}_{P}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\,&\textrm{ if }{N^{\alpha}}\textrm{ is in a red layer;}\end{cases}

•

{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, acting on
\begin{cases}\mathcal{H}_{F}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{in}}}\right)\,&\textrm{ if }{N^{\alpha}}\textrm{ is in a green layer;}\\
\mathcal{H}_{F}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{F}_{i}}({N^{\alpha}})}\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{in}}}\right)\,&\textrm{ if }{N^{\alpha}}\textrm{ is in a red layer;}\end{cases}

such that (once correctly padded) the
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})
’s for different
{N^{\alpha}}
’s all commute pairwise, and the
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
’s commute as well, and such that

\forall{N^{\alpha}},\,{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})=\mathcal{V}_{i}[{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})]\,.

(51)

If
B(i)
and
B(i+1)
are in the same red layer, then all of the former definitions have only been made for the
{N^{\alpha}}
’s in that same layer, i.e. in
\mathcal{L}_{i}(B(i))
. When this happens, we say that
i
is a special step
.

H2 introduces the projectors that will be used to control the status of the still-unfilled branches. The fact that the
\zeta
’s commute pairwise ensures that these controls can always be meaningfully combined. Note that the out-projector for
{N^{\alpha}}
only acts on
{N^{\alpha}}
’s past, while its in-projector only acts on
{N^{\alpha}}
’s future; and furthermore, that for
{N^{\alpha}}
in a green layer its in-projector only acts on
{N^{\alpha}}
’s
strict
future, while for
{N^{\alpha}}
in a red layer its out-projector only acts on its
strict
past. In particular, the
\zeta({N^{\alpha}})
’s never act on
{N^{\alpha}}
itself: this ensures that at any step, a branch never holds some part of its own controls.

We will also write
{\bar{\zeta}_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}}):=\mathbb{1}-{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
and
{\bar{\zeta}_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}}):=\mathbb{1}-{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})
.

Induction Hypothesis 3

(H3)
.

The
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}
’s satisfy

\forall{N^{\alpha}},\forall{O^{\gamma}},{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({O^{\gamma}})\textrm{ acts trivially on }\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\,,

(52)

and the
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}
’s satisfy

\forall{N^{\alpha}},\forall{O^{\gamma}},{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({O^{\gamma}})\textrm{ acts trivially on }\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{in}}}\,.

(53)

This hypothesis encodes the fact that, when a branch
{O^{\gamma}}
doesn’t happen, it doesn’t hold any control on other branches
{N^{\alpha}}
. Note that the
\forall{N^{\alpha}},{O^{\gamma}}
only runs over the branches for which the
\zeta
’s have been defined in H2, i.e. it only runs over
\mathcal{L}_{i}(B(i))
if
i
is a special step. The same will apply in the other hypotheses.

Induction Hypothesis 4

(H4)
.

The
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}
’s satisfy:

\forall{N^{\alpha}},{N^{\beta}},\textrm{ branches of the same node},\,{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\beta}})=0\,.

(54)

The meaning is that two branches of the same node are incompatible. Note that one can infer, using (
51
), that the
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}
’s then satisfy the same property.

Induction Hypothesis 5

(H5)
.

Let
Q\subseteq\{B(i^{\prime})\,|\,i^{\prime}\geq i\}
a set of branches on different nodes; i.e., one can define
\tilde{Q}\subseteq\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}
and a function
\alpha
such that
Q=\{N^{\alpha(N)}\,|\,N\in\tilde{Q}\}
. Then,

\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\circ\prod_{N\in\tilde{Q}}{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}\left(N^{\alpha(N)}\right)=\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(V_{N,i})_{N\in\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}\setminus\tilde{Q}}\times\left(V_{N,i}^{\alpha(N)}\right)_{N\in\tilde{Q}}\right]\,.

(55)

H5 formalises the fact that the
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}
’s control whether branches happen or not. Note that, using (
51
), one could have written the same equation using
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}
’s.

Induction Hypothesis 6

(H6)
.

For a branch
{N^{\alpha}}
in a green layer, we have

\begin{split}&\forall f\in\Lin[\mathcal{H}_{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{in}}}],\,\exists f^{\prime}\in\Lin\left[\mathcal{H}_{P}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\right]\,\textrm{ such that}\\
&\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})={f^{\prime}}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\,.\end{split}

(56)

H6 means that, for a branch
{N^{\alpha}}
in a green layer, provided that one is in the subspace in which branch
{N^{\alpha}}
happens,
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]
’s causal structure only has the
strict
past of
{N^{\alpha}}
signalling to
{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{in}}}
. This will be important to ensure that, when
{N^{\alpha}}
is ‘refilled’, the action of any
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}
’s on it becomes an action on its strict past.

Induction Hypothesis 7

(H7)
.

For a branch
{N^{\alpha}}
in a red layer, we have

\begin{split}&\forall f\in\Lin[\mathcal{H}_{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{out}}}],\,\exists f^{\prime}\in\Lin\left[\mathcal{H}_{F}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{in}}}\right)\right]\,\textrm{ such that}\\
&\mathcal{V}_{i}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})={f^{\prime}}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\,.\end{split}

(57)

H7 plays the same role as H6 in the reverse time direction.

B.4.3
Proof of the base case

H1

The proof that
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}]
is unitary will rely on the lemmas of Section
B.4.1
. To use them, we will first introduce a way to show how bifurcation choices are enforced through the use of the
exch
’s. For every
A
in
\texttt{Arr}_{\Gamma}
, we define
\textsc{witness}_{A}:\mathcal{H}_{A}\to\mathcal{H}_{A}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\absolutevalue{\Ind_A}}
by

\textsc{witness}_{A}:=\sum_{k_{A}\in\texttt{Ind}_{A}}\pi^{k_{A}}_{A}\otimes\ket{k_{A}}\,,

(58)

where the
\pi^{k_{A}}_{A}
’s are the projectors on the different sectors of
A
, and we’ve introduced an arbitrary basis of
\mathbb{C}^{\absolutevalue{\Ind_A}}
labelled by
A
’s index values. The point of
\textsc{witness}_{A}
is simply to channel out the information about each arrow’s index value.

For a given
N
, with respect to the sectorisations of the
\mathcal{H}_{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{out}}}
, of the
\mathcal{H}_{A}
’s for the
A
’s in
\textrm{in}(N)
and
\textrm{out}(N)
, and to the sectorisation of the
\mathbb{C}^{\absolutevalue{\Ind_A}}
’s given by the previous basis,
\lambda_{N}^{\textrm{sec}}
is a route for
\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\textsc{witness}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}
. Thus (because the compatibility with routes is preserved by the dagger compact structure
[
20
]
),
\mathcal{S}^{\mathbf{Rel}}_{\textrm{pad}}[(\lambda_{N}^{\textrm{sec}})_{N}]=\Lambda^{\textrm{sec}}_{\Gamma}
is a route for
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\textsc{witness}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N}\right]
. Therefore,

\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\textsc{witness}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N}\right]\\
&=\sum_{{\vec{k}}\in\bigtimes_{A}\texttt{Ind}_{A}}\left(\bigotimes_{A}\outerproduct{k_A}{k_A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\textsc{witness}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N}\right]\\
&\circ\left(\sum_{\vec{q}\in\left(\Lambda^{\textrm{sec}}\right)^{-1}({\vec{k}})}\bigotimes_{{N^{\alpha}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{q_{N^{\alpha}}}_{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&\overset{\textrm{Lemma \ref {lem: PossVal}}}{=}\sum_{{\vec{k}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}}\left(\bigotimes_{A}\outerproduct{k_A}{k_A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\textsc{witness}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N}\right]\\
&\circ\left(\sum_{\vec{q}\in\left(\Lambda^{\textrm{sec}}\right)^{-1}({\vec{k}})}\bigotimes_{{N^{\alpha}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{q_{N^{\alpha}}}_{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&\overset{\textrm{Lemma \ref {lem: lambdasec}}}{=}\sum_{{\vec{k}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}}\left(\bigotimes_{A}\outerproduct{k_A}{k_A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\textsc{witness}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N}\right]\\
&\circ\left(\bigotimes_{N\in\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\,,\end{split}

(59)

where
\pi^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{out}}}
is the projector on
\mathcal{H}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{out}}}
’s sector labelled by
(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}
(remember that for a given
{N^{\alpha}}
, we have
\mathcal{H}_{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{out}}}=\bigoplus_{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\in\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{{N^{\alpha}}}}\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\mathcal{H}_{A}^{k_{A}}
).

Moreover, we have
\left(\sum_{k\in\texttt{Ind}_{A}}\bra{k}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{witness}_{A}=\mathbb{1}_{A}
, so

\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\\
&=\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\sum_{(k_{A})_{A}\in\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{N}}\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\bra{k_{A}}\circ\textsc{witness}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N}\right]\\
&=\sum_{{\vec{k}}\in\bigtimes_{A}\texttt{Ind}_{A}}\left(\bigotimes_{A}\bra{k_{A}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\left(\textsc{witness}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N}\right]\\
&\overset{\textrm{(\ref {eq: base case H1 1})}}{=}\sum_{{\vec{k}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}}\left(\bigotimes_{A}\bra{k_{A}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\textsc{witness}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N}\right]\\
&\circ\left(\bigotimes_{N\in\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&=\sum_{{\vec{k}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\pi^{k_{A}}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N}\right]\circ\left(\bigotimes_{N\in\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\,.\end{split}

(60)

A symmetric argument relying on
{\Gamma^{\top}}
leads to

\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\\
&=\sum_{{\vec{k}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}}\left(\bigotimes_{N\in\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{in}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\textsc{exch}_{N}\circ\left(\otimes_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}\pi^{k_{A}}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\right)_{N}\right]\,.\end{split}

(61)

Furthermore, the projectors
\left(\bigotimes_{N\in\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}
, for
{\vec{k}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}
, form a sectorisation of the input space of
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]
. Indeed, by Lemma
B.3
, the

\left(\bigtimes_{N}\{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\}_{\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}}}\right)\times\left(\bigtimes_{N^{\alpha}|\alpha\neq{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}}\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{{N^{\alpha}}}\right)

(62)

are the preimage sets of the injective function
\Lambda^{\textrm{sec}}_{\Gamma}
, and therefore form a partition of its domain
\bigtimes_{N^{\alpha}}\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{{N^{\alpha}}}
. The sectorisation is thus obtained as a coarse-graining of that given by the
\bigotimes_{N^{\alpha}}\pi^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}_{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{out}}}
. Symmetrically, the
\left(\bigotimes_{N\in\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{in}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}
form a sectorisation of
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]
’s codomain. Crucially,
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]
is block diagonal with respect to these two sectorisations: indeed, for a given
{\vec{k}}
,

\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\circ\left(\bigotimes_{N\in\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&\overset{\textrm{(\ref {eq: base case H1 2})}}{=}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\pi^{k_{A}}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N}\right]\\
&\overset{\textrm{(\ref {eq:skeletal supermap})}}{=}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\textsc{exch}_{N}\circ\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}\pi^{k_{A}}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\right)_{N}\right]\\
&\overset{\textrm{(\ref {eq: base case H1 2 sym})}}{=}\left(\bigotimes_{N\in\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{in}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\\
\end{split}

(63)

All that is left for us to prove is that all of these blocks, which we will denote as
T^{{\vec{k}}}
’s, are unitary (with respect to the suitable restrictions of their domain and codomain). We start by computing

\begin{split}T^{\vec{k}}&=\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\pi^{k_{A}}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N}\right]\\
&\overset{\textrm{(\ref {eq: def exchange})}}{=}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\pi^{k_{A}}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ i_{N,\textrm{pad}}^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}\circ\left(\textsc{swap}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{in}},N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{out}}}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{\beta\neq{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}}\Theta_{N^{\beta}}\right)\right)\circ p_{N,\textrm{pad}}^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}\right)_{N}\right]\\
&\overset{\textrm{(\ref {eq:skeletal supermap})}}{=}\Tr_{A\in\texttt{Arr}_{\Gamma}^{\textrm{int}}}\left[\bigotimes_{N}\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\pi^{k_{A}}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ i_{N,\textrm{pad}}^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}\circ\textsc{swap}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{in}},N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{out}}}\circ p_{N,\textrm{pad}}^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}\right]\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{M,\beta\neq\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\Theta_{M^{\beta}}\right)\\
&=\left(\left(\bigotimes_{N}p_{N}^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}\right)\circ\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\texttt{Arr}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{k_{A}}_{A}\right)\circ\left(\bigotimes_{N}i_{N}^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}\right)\right)\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{M,\beta\neq\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\Theta_{M^{\beta}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(64)

Remember that
i_{N}^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}
is the injection
\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{out}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{out}}_{N}=\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\mathcal{H}_{A}
, and
p_{N}^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}
is the projection
\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{in}}_{N}=\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}\mathcal{H}_{A}\to\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{in}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}}
. We will also define the injection
i_{N}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}:\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\mathcal{H}_{A}^{k_{A}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{out}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}}
, and the projection
p_{N}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}}:\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{in}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}}\to\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}\mathcal{H}_{A}^{k_{A}}
: these map
T^{\vec{k}}
’s to the suitable domains and codomains. Note that we then have

\bigotimes_{N}i_{N}^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}\circ i_{N}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}=\bigotimes_{A}i_{A}^{k_{A}}\,,

(65a)

\bigotimes_{N}p_{N}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}\circ p_{N}^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}=\bigotimes_{A}p_{A}^{k_{A}}\,,

(65b)

where
i_{A}^{k_{A}}
is the injection
\mathcal{H}_{A}^{k_{A}}\to\mathcal{H}_{A}
and
p_{A}^{k_{A}}
is the projection
\mathcal{H}_{A}\to\mathcal{H}_{A}^{k_{A}}
. Thus,

\begin{split}&\left(\bigotimes_{N}p_{N}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ T^{\vec{k}}\circ\left(\bigotimes_{N}i_{N}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&=\left(\bigotimes_{A}p_{A}^{k_{A}}\circ\pi_{A}^{k_{A}}\circ i_{A}^{k_{A}}\right)\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{M,\beta\neq\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\Theta_{M^{\beta}}\right)\\
&=\left(\bigotimes_{A}\mathbb{1}_{A^{k_{A}}}\right)\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{M,\beta\neq\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\Theta_{M^{\beta}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(66)

Each of the blocks composing
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]
is thus unitary once restricted to the suitable subspaces, so
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]
is unitary as well.

H2

We define, for all branches
{N^{\alpha}}
,

Z^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}}):=\Lambda_{\Gamma}^{-1}\left(\{1\}_{\texttt{Happens}_{N^{\alpha}}}\times\bigtimes_{{M^{\beta}}\neq{N^{\alpha}}}{\texttt{Happens}_{M^{\beta}}}\right)\,,

(67a)

{\zeta^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}}):=\sum_{(l_{M^{\beta}})_{{M^{\beta}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\in Z^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}})}\left(\bigotimes_{M^{\beta}}\pi^{l_{M^{\beta}}}_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\right)\,,

(67b)

and similarly for the
Z^{\textrm{in}}
’s and
{\zeta^{\textrm{in}}}
’s. Note that

Z^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}})=\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\vec{k}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}\\
{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}=\alpha\end{subarray}}\left(\Lambda^{\textrm{sec}}_{\Gamma}\right)^{-1}\left({\vec{k}}\right)\,.

(68)

Given their definition, the
{\zeta^{\textrm{out}}}
’s are commuting orthogonal projectors. Furthermore, as green dashed arrows in
\Gamma^{\texttt{Bran}}
represent
\Lambda_{\Gamma}
’s causal structure, we have, for any branch
{N^{\alpha}}
,

Z^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}}):=\tilde{Z}^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}})\times\left(\bigtimes_{\exists\textrm{ no green dashed arrow }{M^{\beta}}\to{N^{\alpha}}}\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{M^{\beta}}\right)

(69)

Through (
67b
), this implies that
{\zeta^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})
acts trivially on the
{M^{\beta}}
’s that are not linked to
{N^{\alpha}}
by a green dashed arrow. We can thus in particular see it as the padding of an operator acting only on
\mathcal{P}({N^{\alpha}})
, or on
\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})
if
{N^{\alpha}}
is in a red layer. The same applies symmetrically for the
{\zeta^{\textrm{in}}}
’s. Finally, (
63
) implies

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\circ{\zeta^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\vec{k}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}\\
{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}=\alpha\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\circ\left(\bigotimes_{N\in\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\vec{k}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}\\
{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}=\alpha\end{subarray}}\left(\bigotimes_{N\in\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\\
&={\zeta^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\,;\\
\end{split}

(70)

thus,
\mathcal{V}_{0}[{\zeta^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})]={\zeta^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
.

H3

We will prove that
Z^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}})\cap\bar{Z}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})
is of the form
\tilde{Z}\times\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{M^{\beta}}
, from which (H3) derives. This set can be computed, using bra-ket notations in
\mathbf{Rel}
, as
\left(\bra{1}_{{\texttt{Happens}_{N^{\alpha}}}}\otimes\bra{0}_{\texttt{Happens}_{M^{\beta}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\Lambda_{\Gamma}
. Yet, one can see from the definition of the
\lambda^{\textrm{aug}}
’s that

\bra{0}_{{\texttt{Happens}_{M^{\beta}}},\textrm{pad}}\circ\lambda_{M}^{\textrm{aug}}=\bra{0}_{{\texttt{Happens}_{M^{\beta}}},\textrm{pad}}\circ\lambda_{M}^{\textrm{aug}}\circ\outerproduct{\Indout_\Mbe}{\Indout_\Mbe}_{\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}\,;

(71)

thus,

\begin{split}&\left(\bra{1}_{{\texttt{Happens}_{N^{\alpha}}}}\otimes\bra{0}_{\texttt{Happens}_{M^{\beta}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\Lambda_{\Gamma}\\
&=\left(\bra{1}_{{\texttt{Happens}_{N^{\alpha}}}}\otimes\bra{0}_{\texttt{Happens}_{M^{\beta}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}^{\mathbf{Rel}}_{\Gamma}\left[(\lambda_{N}^{\textrm{aug}})_{N}\right]\\
&=\left(\bra{1}_{{\texttt{Happens}_{N^{\alpha}}}}\otimes\bra{0}_{\texttt{Happens}_{M^{\beta}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}^{\mathbf{Rel}}_{\Gamma}\left[(\lambda_{N}^{\textrm{aug}})_{N\neq M}\times\left(\lambda_{M}^{\textrm{aug}}\circ\outerproduct{\Indout_\Mbe}{\Indout_\Mbe}_{\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}\right)\right]\\
&=\left(\left(\bra{1}_{{\texttt{Happens}_{N^{\alpha}}}}\otimes\bra{0}_{\texttt{Happens}_{M^{\beta}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}^{\mathbf{Rel}}_{\Gamma}\left[(\lambda_{N}^{\textrm{aug}})_{N}\right]\circ\ket{\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{M^{\beta}}}_{\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{M^{\beta}}}\right)\otimes\bra{\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{M^{\beta}}}_{\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{M^{\beta}}}\,,\end{split}

(72)

which shows that indeed
Z^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}})\cap\bar{Z}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})=\tilde{Z}\times\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{M^{\beta}}
. The proof for the
Z^{\textrm{in}}
’s is symmetric.

H4

(H4) comes from the fact that, for
\alpha\neq\beta
, one has
Z^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}})\cap Z^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\beta}})=\emptyset
, which can be derived directly from (
68
).

H5

We take
Q=\{N^{\alpha(N)}\,|\,N\in\tilde{Q}\}\subseteq\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}
. Then,

\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\circ\prod_{N\in\tilde{Q}}{\zeta^{\textrm{out}}}(N^{\alpha(N)})\\
&\overset{\textrm{(}\ref {eq: Z from Lasec})}{=}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\vec{k}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}\\
\forall N\in\tilde{Q},{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}=\alpha(N)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\circ\left(\bigotimes_{N\in\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}}_{N^{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&\overset{\textrm{(}\ref {eq: base case H1 2})}{=}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\vec{k}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}\\
\forall N\in\tilde{Q},{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}=\alpha(N)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\pi^{k_{A}}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N}\right]\\
&=\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N\not\in\tilde{Q}}\times\left(\sum_{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\in\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{{N^{\alpha}}}}\left(\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{out}(N)}\pi^{k_{A}}_{A}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N\in\tilde{Q}}\right]\\
&=\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N\not\in\tilde{Q}}\times\left(\pi^{\alpha}_{N^{\textrm{out}}}\circ\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N\in\tilde{Q}}\right]\\
&\overset{\textrm{(}\ref {eq: def exchange})}{=}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[\left(\textsc{exch}_{N}\right)_{N\not\in\tilde{Q}}\times\left(V_{0,N}^{\alpha}\right)_{N\in\tilde{Q}}\right]\,.\end{split}

(73)

H6

We take
{N^{\alpha}}
in a green layer, and
f\in\Lin\left[\mathcal{H}_{{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{in}}}}\right]
. We then have (note that
{\zeta^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
doesn’t act on
{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{in}}}
)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{V}_{0}^{\dagger}\left[f_{\textrm{pad}}\right]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})=\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]^{\dagger}\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
&\overset{\textrm{(}\ref {eq: base zein/zeout})}{=}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]^{\dagger}\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
&=\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]^{\dagger}\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
&\overset{\textrm{(}\ref {eq: base zein/zeout})}{=}{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]^{\dagger}\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
&\overset{\textrm{(}\ref {eq: zeta base}),(\ref {eq: Z from Lasec})}{=}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\vec{k}},{\vec{l}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}\\
{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}={\mu_{N}\left({\vec{l}}\right)}=\alpha\end{subarray}}\left(T^{\vec{l}}\,\right)^{\dagger}\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ T^{\vec{k}}\,.\end{split}

(74)

Furthermore, taking
{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, because
{N^{\alpha}}
is in a green layer we know that there is no red dashed arrow from
{M^{\beta}}
to
{N^{\alpha}}
, and thus
{\zeta^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})
doesn’t act on
{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{in}}}
. We can thus apply the same computation to it as well, which leads to

\begin{split}&\mathcal{V}_{0}^{\dagger}\left[f_{\textrm{pad}}\right]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})=\mathcal{V}_{0}\left[f_{\textrm{pad}}\right]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\left({\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})+{\bar{\zeta}_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\right)\\
&={\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\mathcal{V}_{0}\left[f_{\textrm{pad}}\right]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\\
&+{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\mathcal{V}_{0}\left[f_{\textrm{pad}}\right]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\vec{k}},{\vec{l}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}\\
{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}={\mu_{N}\left({\vec{l}}\right)}=\alpha\\
\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})=\beta\iff\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})=\beta\end{subarray}}\left(T^{\vec{l}}\,\right)^{\dagger}\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ T^{\vec{k}}\,;\end{split}

(75)

in other words, in the sum above, the values of
{\vec{k}}
and
{\vec{l}}
that lead to attributing different statuses to
{M^{\beta}}
correspond to null terms, so that one can skip them in the summation. More generally, one can apply this reasoning to all branches
{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, leading to

\mathcal{V}_{0}\left[f_{\textrm{pad}}\right]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\vec{k}},{\vec{l}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}\\
{\mu_{N}\left({\vec{k}}\right)}={\mu_{N}\left({\vec{l}}\right)}=\alpha\\
\forall{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}}),\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})=\beta\iff\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})=\beta\end{subarray}}\left(T^{\vec{l}}\,\right)^{\dagger}\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ T^{\vec{k}}\,.

(76)

Using (
66
), we rewrite
T^{\vec{k}}
, for an arbitrary
{\vec{k}}
, as

\begin{split}T^{\vec{k}}&=\left(\bigotimes_{M}{p_{M}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}}}\right)^{\dagger}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A}\mathbb{1}_{A^{k_{A}}}\right)\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{M,\beta\neq\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\Theta_{M^{\beta}}\right)\right)\\
&\circ\left(\bigotimes_{M}{i_{M}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(M)}}}\right)^{\dagger}_{\textrm{pad}}\,.\end{split}

(77)

Now, we take
{\vec{k}},{\vec{l}}\in\texttt{PossVal}_{\Gamma}
satisfying the requirements we pinned down earlier; we can then compute

\begin{split}&{T^{\vec{l}}}^{\dagger}\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ T^{\vec{k}}\\
&=\left(\bigotimes_{M}i_{M}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(M)}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A}\mathbb{1}_{A^{l_{A}}}\right)\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{M,\beta\neq\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})}\Theta_{M^{\beta}}^{\dagger}\right)\right)\\
&\circ\left(\bigotimes_{M}{p_{M}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\left(\bigotimes_{M}{p_{M}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}}}\right)^{\dagger}_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&\circ\left(\left(\bigotimes_{A}\mathbb{1}_{A^{{k_{A}}}}\right)\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{M,\beta\neq\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\Theta_{M^{\beta}}\right)\right)\circ\left(\bigotimes_{M}{i_{M}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(M)}}}\right)^{\dagger}_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&=\left(\bigotimes_{M}i_{M}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(M)}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\left[\left(p_{N}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}}\circ f\circ\left(p_{N}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}}\right)^{\dagger}\right)\right.\\
&\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\neq N\\
\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})\neq\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})\end{subarray}}\left(\Theta_{M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}}^{\dagger}\circ\left(p_{M}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}}\right)^{\dagger}\right)\otimes\left(p_{M}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}}\circ\Theta_{M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})}}\right)\right)\\
&\otimes\left.\left(\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\neq N\\
\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})=\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})=1\end{subarray}}{p_{M}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}}}\circ\left(p_{M}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}}\right)^{\dagger}\right)\right]_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\left(\bigotimes_{M}{i_{M}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(M)}}}\right)^{\dagger}_{\textrm{pad}}\,.\end{split}

(78)

Note that each of the
{p_{M}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}}}\circ\left(p_{M}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}}\right)^{\dagger}
terms, for
M
such that
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\cup\{{N^{\alpha}}\}
, can be rewritten as
\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}p_{A}^{l_{A}}\circ i_{A}^{k_{A}}
(which is the identity if
k_{A}=l_{A}\forall A\in\textrm{in}(M)
, and zero otherwise).

Now, for any
M
such that
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, and for any
O
such that
O^{\mu_{O}({\vec{k}})}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\cup\{{N^{\alpha}}\}
, there is no arrow
A\in\texttt{Link}(M,O)
such that
\absolutevalue{A^\kA}\neq 1
, as that would imply the existence of a solid arrow from
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}
to
O^{\mu_{O}({\vec{k}})}
, which would contradict
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
. Thus, all of the non-trivial arrows in
\textrm{out}(M)
go to
O
’s such that
O\neq N
and
\mu_{O}({\vec{l}})=\mu_{O}({\vec{k}})
. Thus this implies that, if one doesn’t have
k_{A}=l_{A}\forall A\in\textrm{out}(M)
then the whole expression is null; while otherwise, the term in square brackets acts trivially on each of the
A\in\textrm{out}(M)
– in other words, the arrows coming out of
M
are never acted on and simply link
i_{M}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(M)}}{}^{\dagger}
directly to
i_{M}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(M)}}
. One can thus reorganise this expression (neglecting the existence of all the trivial spaces) as

\begin{split}&{T^{\vec{l}}}^{\dagger}\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ T^{\vec{k}}=\left[\left(\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\\
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\end{subarray}}i_{M}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(M)}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\left[\vphantom{\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\\
b\\
c\end{subarray}}}\left(p_{N}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}}\circ f\circ\left(p_{N}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}}\right)^{\dagger}\right)\right.\right.\\
&\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\\
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})},M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})\neq\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})\end{subarray}}\left(\Theta_{M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}}^{\dagger}\circ\left(p_{M}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}}\right)^{\dagger}\right)\otimes\left(p_{M}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}}\circ\Theta_{M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})}}\right)\right)\\
&\otimes\left.\left(\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\\
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})},M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})=\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})\end{subarray}}\bigotimes_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}p_{A}^{l_{A}}\circ i_{A}^{k_{A}}\right)\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}O\\
O^{\mu_{O}({\vec{k}})}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\end{subarray}}\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\\
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\end{subarray}}\bigotimes_{A\in\texttt{Link}(M,O)}p_{A}^{l_{A}}\circ i_{A}^{k_{A}}\right)\right]\\
&\circ\left.\left(\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\\
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\end{subarray}}{i_{M}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(M)}}}\right)^{\dagger}_{\textrm{pad}}\right]_{\textrm{pad}}\otimes\left(\left(\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\\
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\end{subarray}}\pi^{({k_{A}})_{A\in\textrm{out}(M)}}_{M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}M,\beta\neq\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})\\
{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{1}_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\right)\right)\,;\end{split}

(79)

note how the action on the
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
’s for
{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
now only consists of a projector independent of
f
. Note that in the second bracket of the third line, the condition
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
could equivalently have been replaced with
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, because we know that
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\implies M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})}=M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, and conversely; so that we have the equivalence
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\iff M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
.

Both bracketed terms in the third line can be rewritten simply as Kronecker deltas, of the form:

\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\\
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})},M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
\mu_{M}({\vec{l}})=\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})\end{subarray}}\prod_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}\delta_{k_{A},l_{A}}\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}O\\
O^{\mu_{O}({\vec{k}})}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\end{subarray}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\\
M^{\mu_{M}({\vec{k}})}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\end{subarray}}\prod_{A\in\texttt{Link}(M,O)}\delta_{k_{A},l_{A}}\right)\,.

(80)

We now take
\vec{q}=(q^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}},\vec{r}=(r^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\in\bigtimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\texttt{Ind}^{\textrm{out}}_{O^{\gamma}}
. We then have

\begin{split}&\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{r^{O^{\gamma}}}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\circ\mathcal{V}_{0}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{q^{O^{\gamma}}}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\\
&=\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{r^{O^{\gamma}}}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]^{\dagger}\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}\left[(\textsc{exch}_{N})_{N}\right]\circ\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{q^{O^{\gamma}}}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
&=\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{r^{O^{\gamma}}}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\circ\left(T^{\Lambda^{\textrm{sec}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{r}\right)}\right)^{\dagger}\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ T^{\Lambda_{\Gamma}^{\textrm{sec}}\left(\vec{q}\right)}\circ\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{q^{O^{\gamma}}}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\,.\end{split}

(81)

Note that
\mu_{M}\circ\Lambda_{\Gamma}^{\textrm{sec}}\left(\vec{q}\right)
denotes the only branch
\beta
of
M
such that the
{M^{\beta}}
term of
\Lambda_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)
, which we denote
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)
, is
1
. By the previous considerations, the term above can thus be non null only if
\Lambda^{N^{\alpha}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=\Lambda^{N^{\alpha}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{r}\right)=1
and if
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{r}\right)\forall{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
. Furthermore, when this is the case, then we can use (
79
) and get

\begin{split}&\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{r^{O^{\gamma}}}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\circ\mathcal{V}_{0}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{q^{O^{\gamma}}}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\\
&=F^{(r^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})},(q^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\pi^{q^{M^{\beta}}}_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\\
&\cdot\prod_{{O^{\gamma}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\delta_{q^{O^{\gamma}},r^{O^{\gamma}}}\,,\end{split}

(82)

where

\begin{split}&F^{(r^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})},(q^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}}\\
&=\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\pi^{r^{O^{\gamma}}}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\circ\left(\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}{M^{\beta}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{r}\right)=1\end{subarray}}i_{M}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(M)}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\left[\vphantom{\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\\
b\\
c\end{subarray}}}\left(p_{N}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}}\circ f\circ\left(p_{N}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(N)}}\right)^{\dagger}\right)\right.\\
&\otimes\left.\left(\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\textrm{ s.t.\ }\exists\beta,\beta^{\prime}:\\
{M^{\beta}},M^{\beta^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=\Lambda^{M^{\beta^{\prime}}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{r}\right)=1\end{subarray}}\left(\Theta_{{M^{\beta}}}^{\dagger}\circ\left(p_{M}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}}\right)^{\dagger}\right)\otimes\left(p_{M}^{(l_{A})_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}}\circ\Theta_{M^{\beta^{\prime}}}\right)\right)\right]\\
&\circ\left(\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}{M^{\beta}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=1\end{subarray}}{i_{M}^{(k_{A})_{A\in\textrm{out}(M)}}}\right)^{\dagger}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\pi^{q^{O^{\gamma}}}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}{M^{\beta}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{r}\right)=\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{A\in\textrm{in}(M)}\delta_{k_{A},l_{A}}\right)\\
&\cdot\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}O\\
\sum_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\Lambda^{O^{\gamma}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=0\end{subarray}}\quad\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\\
\sum_{{M^{\beta}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=1\end{subarray}}\quad\prod_{A\in\texttt{Link}(M,O)}\delta_{k_{A},l_{A}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(83)

with
{\vec{k}}:=\Lambda_{\Gamma}^{\textrm{sec}}(\vec{q})
,
{\vec{l}}:=\Lambda_{\Gamma}^{\textrm{sec}}(\vec{r})
. Note how
F^{(r^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})},(q^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}}
, which comes from the term in square brackets in (
79
) and acts only on
P
and on
{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, doesn’t depend on the value of
(q^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}
: indeed, the values of
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)
and of
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{r}\right)
don’t depend on the values of the
q^{O^{\gamma}}
for
{O^{\gamma}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, as there are no green dashed arrows from these
{O^{\gamma}}
’s to any
{M^{\beta}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
. Similarly, the relevant values of the
k_{A}
’s are those such that
A\in\textrm{out}(M)
for some
{M^{\beta}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
such that
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=1
; thus they are just equal to the
A
-value of
q^{M^{\beta}}
. The same goes for the
l_{A}
’s.

So far, we proved that (
82
) holds for
\vec{q}
and
\vec{r}
satisfying:
\Lambda^{N^{\alpha}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=\Lambda^{N^{\alpha}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{r}\right)=1
and
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{r}\right)\forall{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
. We now want to prove that the same holds when the latter condition is not satisfied – or in other words, that in this case the RHS is also null. We will thus prove that if
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{r}\right)\forall{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
does not hold, then (
83
) is null, and thus the RHS in (
82
) is null as well.

We suppose (
83
) is not null, and take
{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
such that
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=1
. Taking
A\in\textrm{out}(M)
, and denoting
O:=\texttt{head}(A)
and
\gamma
such that
\Lambda^{O^{\gamma}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=1
, we have: either
{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, in which case
k_{A}=l_{A}
by the penultimate term in (
83
); or
{O^{\gamma}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, in which case
k_{A}=l_{A}
by the last term in (
83
). Thus we have
k_{A}=l_{A}\forall A\in\textrm{out}(M)
, and thus
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{r}\right)=1
. Symmetrically,
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{r}\right)=1
implies
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=1
, so we indeed get
\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{r}\right)=\Lambda^{M^{\beta}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)
.

Therefore, (
82
) holds for
\vec{q}
satisfying
\Lambda^{N^{\alpha}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=1
. We can thus finally compute

\begin{split}&\mathcal{V}_{0}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{q},\vec{r}\\
\Lambda^{N^{\alpha}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=1\end{subarray}}\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{r^{O^{\gamma}}}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\circ\mathcal{V}_{0}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\pi^{q^{O^{\gamma}}}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\\
&\overset{\textrm{(}\ref {eq: comp H6 base 3})}{=}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{q},\vec{r}\\
\Lambda^{N^{\alpha}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=1\end{subarray}}F^{(r^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})},(q^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}}\\
&\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\pi^{q^{M^{\beta}}}_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\cdot\prod_{{O^{\gamma}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\delta_{q^{O^{\gamma}},r^{O^{\gamma}}}\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(q^{O^{\gamma}}\right)_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\\
\left(r^{O^{\gamma}}\right)_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\end{subarray}}\left(F^{(r^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})},(q^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}}\otimes\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(q^{O^{\gamma}}\right)_{{O^{\gamma}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\\
\Lambda^{N^{\alpha}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{q}\right)=1\end{subarray}}\left(\bigotimes_{{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\pi^{q^{M^{\beta}}}_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)\right)\\
&=\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(q^{O^{\gamma}}\right)_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\\
\left(r^{O^{\gamma}}\right)_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\end{subarray}}F^{(r^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})},(q^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}}\right)\circ\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(s^{O^{\gamma}}\right)_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\texttt{Bran}_{\Gamma}}\\
\Lambda^{N^{\alpha}}_{\Gamma}\left(\vec{s}\right)=1\end{subarray}}\bigotimes_{{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\pi^{s^{M^{\beta}}}_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&=\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(q^{O^{\gamma}}\right)_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\\
\left(r^{O^{\gamma}}\right)_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}\end{subarray}}F^{(r^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})},(q^{O^{\gamma}})_{{O^{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})}}\right)\circ{\zeta_{\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\,;\end{split}

(84)

denoting the left-hand factor as
f^{\prime}
yields (
56
).

H7

The proof of (H7) is the symmetric of that of (H6).

B.4.4
Proof of the induction step

We suppose the induction hypotheses are all satisfied up to step
i
. We write
{M^{\beta}}:=B(i+1)
, the branch we have to refill in this induction step.

We first consider the case: neither
i
nor
i+1
are special steps. Note that, because the branches have been ordered so that all branches of a same layer are next to each other, the fact that
i+1
is not a special step entails:
{M^{\beta}}
is in a red layer
\implies{\mathcal{P}_{i}}({M^{\beta}})=\emptyset
.

H1

Let us first prove H1 at step
i+1
. From (
51
) and (H5) applied to
Q=\{{M^{\beta}}\}
, we have

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]&=\,{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\neq M}\times(V_{M,i}^{\beta})]\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\\
&+\,{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\neq M}\times(\bar{V}_{M,i}^{\beta})]\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\,.\end{split}

(85)

Furthermore,

\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\neq M}\times(V_{M,i+1}^{\beta})]=\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}[U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\neq M}\times(V_{M,i}^{\beta})]]\,,

(86a)

\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\neq M}\times(\bar{V}_{m,i+1}^{\beta})]=\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}})}\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}[\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\neq M}\times(\bar{V}_{M,i}^{\beta})]]\,,

(86b)

so, because
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})
doesn’t act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
, and
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})
doesn’t act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
,

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]&=\,\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\neq M}\times(V_{M,i+1}^{\beta})]+\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\neq M}\times(\bar{V}_{M,i+1}^{\beta})]\\
&=\,{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}[U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\neq M}\times(V_{M,i}^{\beta})]]\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\\
&+\,{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}})}\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}[\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\neq M}\times(\bar{V}_{M,i}^{\beta})]]\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\,.\end{split}

(87)

Therefore,
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]
can be decomposed into two terms: one that can be considered as a linear map from the subspace of
\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\textrm{out}}
defined by the projector
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})
, to the subspace of
\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\textrm{in}}
defined by the projector
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})
; and one that can be considered a linear map from, and to, the subspaces orthogonal to these. We now have to prove that each of these two terms is unitary.

We start with the first term. (H6) implies that
U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\neq M}\times(V_{M,i}^{\beta})]
features no causal influence from
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
to
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
, via the characterisation of causal influence in terms of algebras
[
37
]
(note that it makes sense to talk about the factors
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
and
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
of its input and output spaces because the
\zeta({M^{\beta}})
’s do not act on these). Therefore, one can find a unitary causal decomposition of it as
W^{2}\circ(\textsc{swap}_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}},{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}}\otimes\mathbb{1})\circ W^{1}
, where
W^{1}
doesn’t act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
and
W^{2}
doesn’t act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
. The first term in (
87
) – with its input and output spaces suitably restricted – is thus of the form
W^{2}\circ(U^{\beta}\otimes\mathbb{1})\circ W^{1}
, which is unitary.

As for the second term, one can see from the definition of the
V_{M,i}^{\alpha}
’s that
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\neq M}\times(\bar{V}_{M,i}^{\beta})]
is of the form
\Theta_{{M^{\beta}}}\otimes W
, with
W
a unitary (once restricted to the suitable subspaces). Therefore, the term can simply be rewritten as
W
.

We have therefore proven (H1) at rank
i+1
.

A Lemma.

Before turning to the other induction hypotheses, we prove a Lemma that we will need to use a few times to compute how
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}
acts on sufficiently well-behaved linear operators.

Lemma B.5
.

Let
g\in\Lin[\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\textrm{in}}]
, not acting (i.e. acting trivially) on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
, commuting with
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})
, and satisfying:
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[g]\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})
doesn’t act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
. We fix an orthonormal (with respect to the Hilbert-Schmidt inner product) basis
(E_{m})_{1\leq m\leq\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}})^{2}}
of
\Lin[\mathcal{H}_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}]
, with
E_{0}=\mathbb{1}
, and decompose
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[g]
as

\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[g]=\sum_{m}\chi_{m}\otimes E_{m}\,,

(88)

with the
\chi_{m}
’s acting on
\mathcal{H}_{P}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}>B(i+1)}\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)
. With padding, we can also write
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[g]=\sum_{m}\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ E_{m,\textrm{pad}}
, with the terms commuting. We then have

\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[g]=\chi_{0,\textrm{pad}}+\sum_{m\neq 0}E^{\prime}_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\,,

(89)

where the
E^{\prime}_{m}
’s are defined, through the use of (H6) at step
i
, by
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[({U^{\beta}}^{\dagger}E_{m}U^{\beta})_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})=E^{\prime}_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})
, with the
E^{\prime}_{m}
’s only acting on
\mathcal{H}_{P}
(because
{\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({M^{\beta}})=\emptyset
).

Proof.

We will compute
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[g]
by looking at how
g
‘moves through’
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]
. First, we rewrite (
87
) more compactly as

\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]\\
&=\,\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[\left({\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\otimes U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}+{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\otimes\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}})}\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\right)\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\right]\,.\end{split}

(90)

Thus (because
g
doesn’t act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
, and commutes with
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})
),

\begin{split}&g\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]\\
&=\,g\circ\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[\left({\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}+{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}})}\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\right)\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\right]\\
&=\,\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[\left({\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}+{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}})}\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\right)\circ g\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\right]\\
&=\,\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[\left({\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}+{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}})}\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\right)\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\circ\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[g]\right]\,.\end{split}

(91)

We now consider the decomposition (
88
) of
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[g]
, and we look at

\begin{split}\sum_{m\neq 0}(\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}}))\otimes E_{m}&=\sum_{m\neq 0}(\chi_{m}\otimes E_{m})_{\textrm{pad}}\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\\
&=\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[g]\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})-(\chi_{0}\otimes\mathbb{1}_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}})_{\textrm{pad}}\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\,.\end{split}

(92)

Both terms of the second line’s RHS act trivially on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
: the first term by assumption, and the second because it is a composition of operators acting trivially on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
. From the form of the LHS, we can thus deduce:
\forall m\neq 0,\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})=0
, which can be rewritten as

\forall m\neq 0,\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}=\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\,.

(93)

In the same way we can prove that
\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}={\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}
. We are now in a position to continue the computation started in (
91
); we write
\kappa_{i}^{\textrm{in}}({M^{\beta}}):={\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\otimes U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}+{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\otimes\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}})}\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}
.

\begin{split}&g\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]\\
&\overset{(\ref {eq: absorbing zeta})}{=}\,\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[\left({\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}+{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}})}\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\right)\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\circ\chi_{0,\textrm{pad}}\right]\\
&+\,\sum_{m\neq 0}\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\circ\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ E_{m,\textrm{pad}}\right]\\
&=\,\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]\circ\chi_{0,\textrm{pad}}\\
&+\,\sum_{m\neq 0}\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[E_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\circ\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\right]\\
&=[\ldots]+\sum_{m\neq 0}\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\circ\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[({U^{\beta}}^{\dagger}E_{m}U^{\beta})_{\textrm{pad}}]\right]\circ\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\\
&=[\ldots]+\sum_{m\neq 0}\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}({M^{\beta}})\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\circ\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[({U^{\beta}}^{\dagger}E_{m}U^{\beta})_{\textrm{pad}}]\right]\circ\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\\
&=[\ldots]+\sum_{m\neq 0}\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\circ\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[({U^{\beta}}^{\dagger}E_{m}U^{\beta})_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\right]\circ\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\\
&\overset{\textrm{(H6)}}{=}[\ldots]+\sum_{m\neq 0}\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\circ E^{\prime}_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\right]\circ\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\\
&=\,[\ldots]+\sum_{m\neq 0}\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\right]\circ E^{\prime}_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\\
&\overset{(\ref {eq: compact rewriting})}{=}\,[\ldots]+\sum_{m\neq 0}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]\circ E^{\prime}_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\\
&\overset{(\ref {eq: absorbing zeta})}{=}\,[\ldots]+\sum_{m\neq 0}\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]\circ E^{\prime}_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\\
&=\,\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]\circ\left(\chi_{0,\textrm{pad}}+\sum_{m\neq 0}E^{\prime}_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(94)

In the previous computation, we used (H6) to replace
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[({U^{\beta}}^{\dagger}E_{m}U^{\beta})_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})
with
E^{\prime}_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})
, with the
E^{\prime}_{m}
’s only acting on
\mathcal{H}_{P}
(because
{\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({M^{\beta}})=\emptyset
). This then allowed to get the term out of the trace. The computation allows us to conclude that (
89
) holds.

∎

H2

We now turn to (H2). We will take:
\forall{N^{\alpha}}>B(i+1),{\zeta_{i+1}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}}):={\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
. There are two things to check in order to ensure that this makes sense. The first is that the
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
are indeed all defined, which holds here because
i
is not a special step. The second thing to check is that for an arbitrary
{N^{\alpha}}
,
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
wasn’t acting on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
. This follows from the fact that
i+1
is not a special step. Indeed, the way we defined the ordering of the branches ensures that
{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
. This ensures that
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
doesn’t act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
if
{N^{\alpha}}
is in a green layer; while if
{N^{\alpha}}
is in a red layer, then the fact that
i+1
is not a special step implies that
{M^{\beta}}
is not in this red layer, i.e. that
{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{F}_{i}}({N^{\alpha}})
and thus that
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
doesn’t act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
.

We then want to define, from there,
{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}}):=\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})],\,\forall{N^{\alpha}}
. The fact (which derives from (H1)) that
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}
is an isomorphism of operator algebras will then ensure that the
{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}
’s are pairwise commuting orthogonal projectors, as the
{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}
’s are. What is left to prove is that, fixing an
{N^{\alpha}}
whose layer is green,
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})]
(which formally acts on the whole
\mathcal{H}_{i+1}^{\textrm{out}}
) can indeed be seen as the padding of an operator acting on
\mathcal{H}_{P}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\mathcal{P}_{i+1}({N^{\alpha}})}\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)
– i.e., that it acts trivially on other factors; and similarly, that for an
{N^{\alpha}}
whose layer is red,
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})]
can be seen as only acting on
\mathcal{H}_{P}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\mathcal{P}_{i+1}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})}\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)
.

For this, fixing an
{N^{\alpha}}>B(i+1)
, we will make use of Lemma
B.5
to compute
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})]
. The latter satisfies the lemma’s assumptions:
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
doesn’t act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
and commutes with
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})
by (H1) at step
i
, and
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})]={\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, by (H3) at step
i
, satisfies:
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})
acts trivially on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
.

By Lemma
B.5
, writing
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})=\sum_{m}\chi_{m}\otimes E_{m}
, we can thus conclude

\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})]=\chi_{0,\textrm{pad}}+\sum_{m\neq 0}E^{\prime}_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ\chi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\,.

(95)

If
{N^{\alpha}}
is in a green layer, then in this expression, the
\chi_{m}
’s act on
\mathcal{H}_{P}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\mathcal{P}_{i+1}({N^{\alpha}})}\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)
and the
E^{\prime}_{m}
’s act on
\mathcal{H}_{P}
; thus,
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})]
only acts non-trivially on
\mathcal{H}_{P}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\mathcal{P}_{i+1}({N^{\alpha}})}\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}\right)
. If
{N^{\alpha}}
is in a red layer, the same can be said replacing
\mathcal{P}
’s with
\mathcal{P}^{\textrm{str}}
’s. This concludes the proof of (H2).

H3

The proof of (H3) is direct for the
{\zeta_{i+1}^{\textrm{in}}}
’s, as they are equal to the
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}
’s. For the
{\zeta_{i+1}^{\textrm{out}}}
’s, fixing
{N^{\alpha}}
and
{O^{\gamma}}
, one can compute
{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({O^{\gamma}})=\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({O^{\gamma}})]
by once again invoking Lemma
B.5
, writing

{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})=\sum_{m}\xi_{m}\otimes E_{m}\,,

(96)

where the
\xi_{m}
’s act trivially on
{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}
because
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})
does, by (H3) at step
i
.
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({O^{\gamma}})
commutes with
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})
and doesn’t act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
; to apply the Lemma, we thus have to prove that
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({O^{\gamma}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})
acts trivially on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
. This follows from the rewriting

\begin{split}&{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({O^{\gamma}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\\
&=\left({\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\right)\circ\left({\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})-{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({O^{\gamma}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\right)\,,\end{split}

(97)

all of the terms in which, one can conclude by (H3) at step
i
, act trivially on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
. Lemma
B.5
thus leads to

{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({O^{\gamma}})=\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({O^{\gamma}})]=\xi_{0,\textrm{pad}}+\sum_{m\neq 0}E^{\prime}_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ\xi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\,,

(98)

with neither the
\xi_{m}
’s nor the
E^{\prime}_{m}
’s acting on
{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}}
, which concludes the proof of (H3).

H4

The proof of (H4) at step
i+1
is immediate as it derives from (H4) at step
i
for the
{\zeta_{i+1}^{\textrm{in}}}
’s, which are equal to the
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}
’s.

H5

For the proof of (H5), we take
Q\subseteq\{B(i^{\prime})\,|\,i^{\prime}\geq i+1\}
a set of branches on different nodes, and
\tilde{Q}\subseteq\texttt{Nodes}_{\Gamma}
and a function
\alpha
such that
Q=\{N^{\alpha(N)}\,|\,N\in\tilde{Q}\}
. We will prove the version of (H5) written with
{\zeta_{i+1}^{\textrm{in}}}
’s. We first consider the case
M\in\tilde{Q}
. Then by (H4) we have
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}(M^{\alpha(M)})={\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}(M^{\alpha(M)})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})
, and we can therefore write

\begin{split}&\prod_{N\in\tilde{Q}}{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}(N^{\alpha(N)})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]\\
&=\,\prod_{N\in\tilde{Q}}{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}(N^{\alpha(N)})\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]\\
&=\,\prod_{N\in\tilde{Q}}{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}(N^{\alpha(N)})\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]\\
&=\,\prod_{N\in\tilde{Q}}{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}(N^{\alpha(N)})\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}}))}\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}\left[\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\right]\\
&=\,\prod_{N\in\tilde{Q}}{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}(N^{\alpha(N)})\circ\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}}))}\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}\left[\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\right]\\
&\overset{\textrm{(H5)}}{=}\,\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}}))}\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}\left[\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\not\in\tilde{Q}}\times(V^{\alpha(N)}_{N,i})_{N\in\tilde{Q}}]\right]\\
&=\,\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}}))}\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}\left[\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\circ\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N\not\in\tilde{Q}}\times(V^{\alpha(N)}_{N,i+1})_{N\in\tilde{Q}}]\right]\\
&=\,\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N\not\in\tilde{Q}}\times(V^{\alpha(N)}_{N,i+1})_{N\in\tilde{Q}}]\,.\end{split}

(99)

In the case
M\not\in\tilde{Q}
, then defining
\alpha(M):=\beta
, we have

\begin{split}&\prod_{N\in\tilde{Q}}{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}(N^{\alpha(N)})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]\\
&=\,\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[\prod_{N\in\tilde{Q}\cup\{M\}}{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}(N^{\alpha(N)})\circ U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\right]\\
&+\,\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}})}\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[\prod_{N\in\tilde{Q}}{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}(N^{\alpha(N)})\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\right]\\
&\overset{\textrm{(H5)}}{=}\,\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\not\in\tilde{Q}\cup\{M\}}\times(V_{N,i}^{\alpha(N)})_{N\in\tilde{Q}\cup\{M\}}]\right]\\
&+\,\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}})}\Tr_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}}\left[\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N\not\in\tilde{Q}\cup\{M\}}\times(V_{N,i}^{\alpha(N)})_{N\in\tilde{Q}}\times(\bar{V}_{M,i}^{\beta})]\right]\\
&=\,\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N\not\in\tilde{Q}\cup\{M\}}\times(V_{N,i+1}^{\alpha(N)})_{N\in\tilde{Q}}\times(V_{M,i}^{\beta})]\\
&+\,\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N\not\in\tilde{Q}\cup\{M\}}\times(V_{N,i+1}^{\alpha(N)})_{N\in\tilde{Q}}\times(\bar{V}_{M,i}^{\beta})]\\
&=\,\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N\not\in\tilde{Q}}\times(V_{N,i+1}^{\alpha(N)})_{N\in\tilde{Q}}]\,.\end{split}

(100)

H6

To prove (H6), we fix a branch
{N^{\alpha}}>B(i+1)
in a green layer, and
f\in\Lin[\mathcal{H}_{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{in}}}]
. We first consider the case
{N^{\alpha}}\not\in{\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({M^{\beta}})
.
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})
then doesn’t act on
{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{in}}}
: indeed, either
{M^{\beta}}
is in a green layer and
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}({M^{\beta}})
doesn’t act outside of
{\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({M^{\beta}})
, or
{M^{\beta}}
is in a red layer and then, because
i+1
is not a special step,
{N^{\alpha}}
is not in this layer and thus
{N^{\alpha}}\not\in{\mathcal{F}_{i}}({M^{\beta}})
. Furthermore, as we saw that
\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N})]\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})
was of the form
W\otimes\Theta_{M^{\beta}}
, and
f
doesn’t act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
, it follows that
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[f]\circ{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})
doesn’t act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
. We can therefore apply Lemma
B.5
and get

\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]=\phi_{0,\textrm{pad}}+\sum_{m\neq 0}E^{\prime}_{m,\textrm{pad}}\circ\phi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\,\overset{\textrm{(H6)}}{=}\sum_{m}\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[({U^{\beta}}^{\dagger}E_{m}U^{\beta})_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ\phi_{m,\textrm{pad}}\,,

(101)

where
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]=\sum_{m}\phi_{m}\otimes E_{m}
. Furthermore, as we’ve seen, we have
