{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})=\sum_{n}\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[({U^{\beta}}^{\dagger}E_{n}U^{\beta})_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ\chi_{n}
. We are therefore led to

\begin{split}&\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})=\sum_{mn}\phi_{m}\circ\chi_{n}\circ\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[({U^{\beta}}^{\dagger}\circ E_{m}\circ E_{n}\circ U^{\beta})_{\textrm{pad}}]\\
&=\,\left(\sum_{mn}\sigma_{lmn}\phi_{m}\circ\chi_{n}\right)\circ\sum_{l}\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[({U^{\beta}}^{\dagger}\circ E_{l}\circ U^{\beta})_{\textrm{pad}}]\,,\end{split}

(102)

where the
\sigma_{lmn}
’s are the structure constants on
\Lin[{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}]
, i.e.
E_{m}\circ E_{n}=\sum_{l}\sigma_{lmn}E_{l}
. Yet (H6) at step
i
gives us that there exists
f^{\prime}
acting on
P
and
{\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
(and therefore not on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
) such that
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})={f^{\prime}}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, which can be rewritten as

\sum_{l}\left(\sum_{mn}\sigma_{lmn}\phi_{m}\circ\chi_{n}\right)\otimes E_{l}=\sum_{l}\left(f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\chi_{l}\right)\otimes E_{l}\,,

(103)

leading to

\forall l,\sum_{mn}\sigma_{lmn}\phi_{m}\circ\chi_{n}=f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\chi_{l}\,.

(104)

Reinserting this into (
102
), we find

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})&=\,f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\sum_{l}\chi_{l}\circ\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[({U^{\beta}}^{\dagger}\circ E_{l}\circ U^{\beta})_{\textrm{pad}}]\\
&=f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\,,\end{split}

(105)

where
f^{\prime}
acts on
P
and on
{\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, and the latter is equal to
\mathcal{P}_{i+1}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})
as
{M^{\beta}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
.

We now consider the case
{N^{\alpha}}\in{\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({M^{\beta}})
. We will use the fact that (
56
) can be equivalently written as
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]=f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})
; indeed,
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
doesn’t act on
{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{in}}}
, so
f_{\textrm{pad}}
and
{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
commute, so
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[f]
and
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})]={\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})
commute as well. We write
\kappa_{i}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}}):={\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\otimes U_{\textrm{pad}}^{\beta}+{\bar{\zeta}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\otimes\frac{1}{\texttt{dim}({M^{\beta}})}\Theta_{{M^{\beta}},\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}
.

\begin{split}&{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]^{\dagger}\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&=\,\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]^{\dagger}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&=\,\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}\left[\kappa^{\textrm{out}}_{i,\textrm{pad}}({M^{\beta}})^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]^{\dagger}\right]\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&=\,\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}\left[\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})^{\dagger}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]^{\dagger}\right]\\
&\overset{\textrm{(H6)}}{=}\,\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}\left[\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})^{\dagger}\circ f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]^{\dagger}\right]\\
&=\,\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}\left[\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})^{\dagger}\circ f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]^{\dagger}\right]\circ{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\,,\\
\end{split}

(106)

from which we get

\begin{split}&{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\\
&=\,{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]^{\dagger}\circ f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]\\
&=\,\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}\left[\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})^{\dagger}\circ f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]^{\dagger}\right]\\
&\circ\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}\left[\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\circ{\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\right]\circ{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\,.\end{split}

(107)

We will rewrite the traces in another way, defining
\ket{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}:=\sum_{k}\ket{k}_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}\otimes\ket{k}_{{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}^{\prime}}
, where
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}^{\prime}\cong{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
, and
(\ket{k})_{k}
is an arbitrary orthonormal basis. The above can then be expressed as

\begin{split}&{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\\
&=\,\bra{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})^{\dagger}\circ f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]^{\dagger}\circ\ket{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&\circ\bra{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]\circ{\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\ket{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
&=\,\bra{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})^{\dagger}\circ f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{V}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\left[\ket{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}_{\textrm{pad}}\bra{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}_{\textrm{pad}}\right]\\
&\circ{\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({M^{\beta}})\circ\ket{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\,.\\
\end{split}

(108)

In this expression,
\bra{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}
and
\ket{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}
act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
and
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}^{\prime}
;
\kappa^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})
acts on
P
,
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
/
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}
(on its domain/codomain), and
{\mathcal{P}_{i}}({M^{\beta}})\subseteq{\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
;
f^{\prime}
acts on
P
and
{\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
; and
\mathcal{V}_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\left[\ket{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}_{\textrm{pad}}\bra{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}_{\textrm{pad}}\right]
acts on
P
,
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
,
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathcal{P}_{i}}({M^{\beta}})\subseteq{\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})
. Their composition – which doesn’t act on
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}
and
{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{out}}}^{\prime}
as these are explicitly terminated by
\bra{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}
and
\ket{\Phi^{+}({M^{\beta}})}
– thus acts trivially outside of
P
and
{\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})\setminus\{{M^{\beta}}\}=\mathcal{P}_{i+1}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})
. Therefore, we can write

{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]=f^{\prime\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\,,

(109)

with
f^{\prime\prime}\in\Lin\left[\mathcal{H}_{P}\otimes(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\mathcal{P}_{i+1}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})}\mathcal{H}_{O^{\gamma}_{\textrm{out}}})\right]
.

H7

We take
{N^{\alpha}}>{M^{\beta}}
in a red layer, and
f\in\Lin[\mathcal{H}_{{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{out}}}}]
. Because
{N^{\alpha}}
is in a red layer and
i+1
is not a special step, we have
{N^{\alpha}}\not\in{\mathcal{P}_{i}}({M^{\beta}})
. Thus
f
commutes with
\kappa_{i}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})
, as the latter only acts non trivially on
P
,
{\mathcal{P}_{i}}({M^{\beta}})
and
{M^{\beta}}
. Thus,

\begin{split}&f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]^{\dagger}\circ{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
&=\,f_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]^{\dagger}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
&=\,\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}\left[\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\right]\\
&=\,\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}\left[\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]^{\dagger}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\right]\\
&\overset{\textrm{(H7)}}{=}\,\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}\left[\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]^{\dagger}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\right]\\
&=\,\Tr_{M^{\beta}_{\textrm{in}}}\left[\kappa_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i})_{N}]^{\dagger}\right]\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\\
&=\,\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]^{\dagger}\circ{\zeta_{i,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\,,\end{split}

(110)

where
f^{\prime}
acts on
{\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}({N^{\alpha}})=\mathcal{F}_{i+1}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})
. We can use this to find (noting that
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}[f]
and
{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
commute because
f
and
{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})
do)

{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{V}_{i+1}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]=\mathcal{V}_{i+1}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\circ{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})={\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\,.

(111)

The special steps

The previous proofs were relying on the assumption that neither
i
nor
i+1
were special steps. We now consider the other cases. Note first that the proof of (H1) presented earlier is valid in these cases as well.

We start with the case:
i+1
is a special step (regardless of the status of
i
). One then only has to define, and check the properties of, the
\zeta_{i+1}({N^{\alpha}})
’s for
{N^{\alpha}}\in\mathcal{L}_{i+1}({M^{\beta}})
. Note that for such
{N^{\alpha}}
’s, the
\zeta_{i}({N^{\alpha}})
’s were defined at step
i
; indeed, either
i
is not a special step and all the
\zeta_{i}
’s were defined, or
i
is a special step, which entails that
\mathcal{L}_{i+1}({M^{\beta}})\subset\mathcal{L}_{i}(B(i))
, and the
\zeta_{i}
’s were defined for elements of this latter set.

One can then follow a proof strategy that is a time-reversed version of the one presented earlier, except that only the
{N^{\alpha}}
’s in
\mathcal{L}_{i+1}({M^{\beta}})
are considered. Namely, we will define, for
{N^{\alpha}}\in\mathcal{L}_{i+1}({M^{\beta}})
,
{\zeta_{i+1}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}}):={\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})
and
{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}}):=\mathcal{V}_{i}[{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})]
, and the rest of the proof can be obtained by following the earlier proof, simply replacing in’s with out’s, looking at daggered versions of maps, etc. Indeed, the induction hypotheses are fully invariant under time-symmetry, except for one crucial thing: the fact that, when looking in the forward direction, all branches of the layers in the strict past of the branch under consideration have been refilled already, and thus have no
\zeta
’s. Here, however, we are only redefining, and proving properties of, the
\zeta_{i+1}({N^{\alpha}})
’s of the layer under consideration; everything thus goes as if the branches in its strict future didn’t exist. Moreover, the fact that
i+1
is a special step implies that
B(i+1)
is in a red layer, which means that, when considering things from a time-reversed perspective,
B(i+1)
is in a green layer and thus neither
i
not
i+1
are special steps. Thus, for the purposes of defining these
\zeta_{i+1}({N^{\alpha}})
’s, the situation is exactly symmetric to the one considered previously.

A final case to consider is:
i
is a special step but
i+1
is not. In this case,
B(i)
and
{M^{\beta}}=B(i+1)
are in the same red layer, but
B(i+2)
and the rest of the
{N^{\alpha}}>B(i+1)
aren’t. The interpretation is that we just finished filling up the branches of a red layer, a procedure during which we didn’t define
\zeta
’s for the branches above it; so that we now have to redefine them. The strategy for this case is to define the
{\zeta_{i+1}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
’s to be equal, not to the
{\zeta_{i}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
’s – which were not defined –, but to the
{\zeta_{j}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})
, where
j
is the latest step that was not special, i.e. the latest step at which these were defined.
B(j)
is then the first branch of the red layer we finished refilling.

One can then follow a strategy similar to the previous proof, now deriving that (H2)-(H7) hold at step
i+1
from the fact that they hold at step
j
. Let us highlight the main steps. First, from (
87
) holding at all steps between
j
and
i+1
, we can deduce

\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,i+1})_{N}]=\Tr_{B(t)^{\textrm{in}},\,j<t\leq i+1}\left[\mathcal{S}_{\textrm{pad}}[(V_{N,j})_{N}]\circ\left(\prod_{t=j+1}^{i+1}\kappa_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}(B(t))\right)\right]\,,

(112)

where we also used the fact that, due to how we defined the
\zeta^{\textrm{out}}
’s to remain the same when filling up a red layer, we have
\forall t\in\llbracket j+1,i+1\rrbracket,\kappa_{t}^{\textrm{out}}(B(t))=\kappa_{j}^{\textrm{out}}(B(t))
.

For (H2), we fix
{N^{\alpha}}>B(i+1)
; note that we then have
{N^{\alpha}}\in{\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\textrm{str}}}(B(i+1))
, because
B(i+1)
is the last branch in its (red) layer. As before, we have to prove that, defining
{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}}):=\zeta_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}
and
{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}}):=\mathcal{V}^{i+1}[{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})]
, the latter doesn’t act outside of
P
and
\mathcal{P}_{i+1}({N^{\alpha}})
(or
\mathcal{P}_{i+1}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})
if
{N^{\alpha}}
is in a red layer). Using (
112
) and techniques similar to before, we are led to

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[\zeta_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}({N^{\alpha}})]&=\left(\prod_{t=j+1}^{i+1}\bra{\phi^{+}(B(t))}_{\textrm{pad}}\right)\circ\left(\prod_{t=j+1}^{i+1}\kappa_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}(B(t))^{\dagger}\right)\\
&\circ\mathcal{V}_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\left[\prod_{t=j+1}^{i+1}\ket{\Phi^{+}(B(t))}_{\textrm{pad}}\bra{\Phi^{+}(B(t))}_{\textrm{pad}}\right]\circ\zeta_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}})\\
&\circ\left(\prod_{t=j+1}^{i+1}\kappa_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}(B(t))\right)\circ\left(\prod_{t=j+1}^{i+1}\ket{\phi^{+}(B(t))}_{\textrm{pad}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(113)

in which
\zeta_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}})
acts only on
P
and
\mathcal{P}_{j}({N^{\alpha}})
(
\mathcal{P}_{j}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})
if
{N^{\alpha}}
is in a red layer), and the other terms act only on
\mathcal{L}(B(i+1))
and on
P
. Given that all the wires in
\mathcal{L}(B(i+1))
are explicitly terminated by the
\phi^{+}
’s, it follows that
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[\zeta_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}({N^{\alpha}})]
only acts on
P
and on
\mathcal{P}_{j}({N^{\alpha}})\setminus\mathcal{L}(B(i+1))=\mathcal{P}_{i+1}({N^{\alpha}})
(or
\mathcal{P}_{j}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})\setminus\mathcal{L}(B(i+1))=\mathcal{P}_{i+1}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})
if
{N^{\alpha}}
is in a red layer).

The proof of (H3) is fully analogous: computing
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[\zeta_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\bar{\zeta}_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}({M^{\beta}})]
leads to (
113
) with
\zeta_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}})
replaced with
\zeta_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\bar{\zeta}_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({M^{\beta}})
, so that invoking (H3) at step
j
leads to (H3) at step
i+1
. (H4), as before, is direct, and the proof of (H5) is analogous to the one for the non-special cases.

For the proof of (H6), we take
{N^{\alpha}}>B(i+1)
in a green layer and
f\in\Lin[\mathcal{H}_{{N^{\alpha}_{\textrm{in}}}}]
. Then, the computation is similar to (
106
), (
107
) and (
108
), yielding

\begin{split}&{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{V}_{i+1}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]\\
&=\left(\prod_{t=j+1}^{i+1}\bra{\phi^{+}(B(t))}_{\textrm{pad}}\right)\circ\left(\prod_{t=j+1}^{i+1}\kappa_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}(B(t))^{\dagger}\right)\circ\mathcal{V}_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\dagger}\left[\prod_{t=j+1}^{i+1}\ket{\Phi^{+}(B(t))}_{\textrm{pad}}\bra{\Phi^{+}(B(t))}_{\textrm{pad}}\right]\\
&\circ f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\left(\prod_{t=j+1}^{i+1}\kappa_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}(B(t))\right)\circ\left(\prod_{t=j+1}^{i+1}\ket{\phi^{+}(B(t))}_{\textrm{pad}}\right)\circ{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}}({N^{\alpha}})\,,\end{split}

(114)

where
f^{\prime}\in\Lin[\mathcal{H}_{P}\otimes(\bigotimes_{{O^{\gamma}}\in\mathcal{P}_{j}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})})]
was defined through
\zeta_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{V}_{j}^{\dagger}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]=f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\circ\zeta_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{out}}({N^{\alpha}})
by using (H6) at step
j
. Once again, by looking at where the operators are acting, we can conclude that this defines a
f^{\prime\prime}
acting only on
P
and on
\mathcal{P}_{j}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})\setminus\mathcal{L}(B(i+1))=\mathcal{P}_{i+1}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})
.

Finally, for (H7), one can follow computations (
110
) and (
111
), to get

{\zeta_{i+1,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ\mathcal{V}_{i+1}[f_{\textrm{pad}}]=\zeta_{j,\textrm{pad}}^{\textrm{in}}({N^{\alpha}})\circ f^{\prime}_{\textrm{pad}}\,,

(115)

where
f^{\prime}
, obtained by the use of (H7) at step
j
, only acts on
\mathcal{F}_{j}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})=\mathcal{F}_{i+1}^{\textrm{str}}({N^{\alpha}})
. This concludes the proof of the induction step.

Conclusion

As the base case and induction step are true, the induction hypotheses are true at every step up to
n
. In particular, (H1) at step
n
then reads:

\mathcal{S}[(U_{N})_{N}]\textrm{ is unitary.}

(116)

As this was done for
\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}_{\left(\Gamma,{({\lambda_{N}})}_{N}\right)}
for an arbitrary valid routed graph
{\left(\Gamma,{({\lambda_{N}})}_{N}\right)}
, and for an arbitrary collection of routed unitaries
U_{N}:\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{in}}_{N}\overset{{\lambda_{N}}}{\to}\mathcal{H}^{\textrm{out}}_{N}
following the
{\lambda_{N}}
’s, we can invoke Lemma
B.2
and conclude that Theorem
B.1
holds.

∎
